> Here is the boom and bust cycle.  
>   
> The banks loan money from nothing, by fiat they print money, but only the money not the interest needed to pay that loan off.  
>   
> Therefore there is always more debt owed than money that exists to pay it.  
>   
> To get access to those loans created from nothing you have to give a security of something real, of something that exists.  
>   
> Then a game of musical chairs starts.  
>   
> The borrowers have to go out into the world and compete for the limited supply of fiat money to pay off their loans;  
>   
> If they can't,   
> the bank claims their security, the real stuff.  
>   
> If they can,  
> the bank keeps the interest as profits.  
>   
> Hence the boom and bust cycle is that critical moment when there is too much debt and the banks have an issue so initiate a harvest of the public.

> ​@isaidpianissimo2475 That’s a brilliant metaphor, and the 'IDK' is the only honest reaction! It actually reminds me of the Ming Dynasty "Philosopher" Wang Yangming and his concept that 'nothing exists outside the mind' (心外無物) — this quote feels like the Western philosophical version of that exact trap. When you stare at a flower, its colors and shapes become vivid in your consciousness (Empiricism). But the moment you turn your head away and remove your perception, you technically can't prove with 100% scientific certainty that the flower is still there (sliding right into Solipsism). The 'IDK' is just us choosing to ignore that lonely frontage road and stay on the main highway so we can keep functioning! The truth is, Solipsism might be a logically bulletproof frontage road, but it's a dead end. If you take that exit, you drive alone and nothing matters.  
>   
> So, we just have to accept the "IDK." We acknowledge that the weird little frontage road is right there, running parallel to us. But to actually live our lives, critique the world, and enjoy the ride, we have to keep our hands tightly on the wheel and stay on the Empiricism highway. Keep driving! >
> That's not how banks work at all. Let's say I bring $1Mil to the bank for a year. The bank would take that money and bring it to the central bank as collateral to take out a loan. The equity ratio usually has to be around 10% although it is often much less. Equity ratio means how much money a bank can take out for it's collateral. With a ratio of 10%, the bank can take out $10Mil in this case. The bank now lends this money to 10 borrowers for $1 million each, charging interest. At an interest rate of 5% and a term of one year, the bank would receive a total of $10.5 million back from its borrowers at the end of the term. The bank would then have to repay the loan it had from the central bank, including interest at 2%. This would amount to a payment of $10.2 million. The bank receives its initial deposit of $1 million back from the central bank. The saver receives their money back at 1% interest, amounting to $1.01 million. After repaying all debts, the bank is left with $290,000 after one year. This sum is a risk premium that the bank has borne throughout the year and will be used if necessary in the event of borrower defaults (if no default happens, it will become the banks equity). So the margin is very thin, if only one borrower out of the ten defaults on only 30% of his loan (only pays back 700k), the bank is 60k under water. Bust is when too many people defaulted on their loans and the banks equity gets wiped out. It then is no longer able to to give out loans which slows down the economy. Saying it is some sort of conspiracy, is like saying that the four seasons are one too. Mr. Jiang, please start valuing inductive thinking more, because that is the exact problem why you don't get these simple mechanisms. You try to come up with theories how the noumenon might work while completely ignoring that the phenomena speak against your theories and that for understanding the workings of things inductive thinking is much more valuable. Because this is a blind spot for you, this will regularly be a problem for you. Read C.G. Jungs book "Psychological Types" to understand what you overlook.

> False claims of professorship are rarely accidental and rarely trivial. Claiming to be a professor when one is not is usually not illegal by itself and risks are minimal, but benefits are significant.
  
> Primary motive: To make audiences trust them faster and question them less.
  
> “Professor” carries strong cultural signals:
  
> Expertise, Peer review, Intellectual rigor, Neutrality or objectivity
  
> By Claiming the title one: Short-circuits skepticism, Elevates opinions into perceived facts, Makes viewers more likely to accept conclusions without independent verification.
  
> This is especially effective on YouTube, where viewers often lack the time or tools to verify credentials.

> 
  
> Conspiracy Theorist:
  
> Evidence is interpreted through suspicion
  
> Absence of evidence = proof of cover‑up
  
> Debunking strengthens belief
  
> The theory expands to absorb contradictions
  
> This creates a self‑sealing belief system.
  
> 
  
> Propagandist:
  
> Evidence is selectively used, omitted, or recontextualized
  
> Contradictions are avoided, ignored, or reframed
  
> Claims are adjusted for effectiveness, not coherence
  
> Consistency matters only insofar as it helps persuasion
  
>   
> The Propagandist:
  
> Often aligned with, funded by, or useful to power
  
> May pretend to be anti‑elite, but messaging serves interests
  
> Rarely questions all institutions—only inconvenient ones
  
>   
> While the Conspiracy Theorist is
  
> Typically anti‑institutional
  
> Positions self as outsider
  
> Claims persecution or censorship
  
> Distrusts all centers of authority
  
> Follow the incentives—not just the claims
  
> 
  
> Conspiracy Theorist
  
> May lose money, reputation, or relationships
  
> Often persists despite negative consequences
  
> Financial gain is inconsistent or secondary  
>   
> Propagandist
  
> Incentives are clear:
  
> Money
  
> Power
  
> Audience growth
  
> Political leverage
  
> Message aligns with revenue or influence channels  
>   
> The scales tip in favor of a propagandist

### **@thinkerds** | 👍 8 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VbowBTJWKBo&lc=UgxrTBgVSLO6Mvttc0R4AaABAg)

In this lecture, Professor Jiang presents a speculative theory arguing that global financial crises are not accidental, but are engineered by a global financial elite to control the economy. He frames this as a 'game' where these elites manipulate the system for profit and power.

### Key Themes of the Lecture:

The Illusion of the 'Boom-Bust' Cycle (0:28 - 2:03): The professor argues that mainstream economics labels these cycles as natural consequences of capitalism, whereas he contends they are mechanisms triggered by insiders to create chaos and consolidate wealth.
The Role of Money and Banking (4:10 - 7:51): He describes money as a 'collective hallucination' and explains how central banks use interest rates not to guide consumer behavior, but as a signaling mechanism to coordinate liquidity among commercial banks.
Transnational Capital (12:08 - 13:25): He identifies the 'game masters' as powerful institutions, including the Bank for International Settlements (BIS), Wall Street, and the City of London, which operate as both parasites and hosts to the global economy.
The 2008 Financial Crisis (26:36 - 36:06): Jiang claims the 2008 crisis was an engineered event that allowed large institutions to consolidate the banking sector and acquire distressed assets—such as residential real estate—on a massive scale.
Shifting Economic Gravity (43:44 - 47:53): He asserts that after 2008, the financial elite orchestrated a shift of economic focus toward China to maintain global trade flows, eventually leading to the current dominance of large Chinese banks.
Future Geopolitical Predictions (50:12 - 58:23): The lecture concludes with the hypothesis that, due to geopolitical friction with China and the United States, the financial elite are preparing to shift the center of global trade and influence toward Israel, potentially involving future conflicts to facilitate this transition.

A boom happens due to excessive growth in bank credit resulting from artificially low interest rates set by central banks. The bust that follows comes when the credit expansion ceases, again due to central bank policy.

### **@brunoseixascorrea**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VbowBTJWKBo&lc=Ugynqey4tZYCt_kIm5l4AaABAg)

Dear Professor Jiang, I find your lessons amazing, and the vast knowledge you provide is a treasure. But I have to humbly disagree when it comes to names like John Locke 19:06 and the Liberal Movement during early Enlightment. Liberalism came to be as a way of fighting the totalitarian and authoritarian regimes in Europe. At that time, when feudalism was turning into mercantilism, everything was perceived as belonging to the king, and this was pretty much the same everywhere. Bourbon Dynasty in France, Ming Dynasty in China, the Mughal in India, Otomans in Turkey, the Aztecs in Mexico, you name it... Power, wealth and property were hereditary by law. So, in my humble opinion, the idea that one should have rights just for being an individual, was a major breakthough in human history. It brought the perception that it doesn't matter which family you were born into, what name you carry, what god you pray to, what color your skin is... you still deserve rights. Now, is it possible that this idea was hijacked and exploited by evil people? Sure. That same happened to Buddhism, Christianity, Socialism... But it doesn't mean the idea itself is bad. It's just misused.

Wrong. When you deposit money into a bank, YOUR money is NEVER loaned out. By law that bank is REQUIRED to pay that money back at any second you ask for it, therefore, loaning a bank YOUR money is a bank debt and a liability because not only do they need to have that money ready to PAY YOU but they owe you interest. The bank assets are in the FEES they charge YOU to pay bills or remove your money called - "transaction fees". Money in the bank that is loaned out is "invented money" or "fiat money". They are allowed to do that by law. The interest YOU pay that bank on borrowing money is "real money", a profit to that bank - because it is backed by YOUR labour or assets. YOUR deposits in a bank are only used as a measurement of that bank's success. The more deposits the more that banks APPEAR to be successful to SHARE HOLDERS. That is ALL your deposits are used for.

"Fiat currency is government-issued money not backed by a physical commodity like gold or silver. Its value is derived from government decree, public trust, and supply-demand dynamics rather than intrinsic worth. It serves as legal tender for all debts and powers most modern economies, including the U.S. dollar, Euro, and CAD." (7) Is this not a Google entry "Yes, banks create and issue the vast majority of fiat money in modern economies, primarily through commercial lending. While central banks issue physical currency (notes and coins), commercial banks create "digital" fiat money whenever they grant loans, increasing the money supply by depositing newly created funds into borrowers' accounts. From USA Yahoo Finance: "The Revolving Money Myth: Your Deposits Don’t Fund Bank Loans
  
> "Common mythology says that banks take the money that customers deposit into their savings accounts and lend it out to borrowers for a profit. ...Banks Create the Money They Lend
  
> Banks don’t need your money to extend loans. The loans themselves create new money.
  
> "Each and every time a bank makes a loan, the laws of double-entry accounting require them to create a new account for the borrower and make a deposit equal to the loan amount. 
  
> "According to Forbes, this means that banks create money every time they lend money. They’re able to do this because banks are allowed to lend much more money than they have. The Money They Lend Isn’t Really There
  
> Banks operate on a system called fractional reserve, which allows them to keep only a small fraction of the money they lend available on hand as withdrawable cash reserves. Also, the 1913 Federal Reserve Act requires banks to maintain the minimum cash reserves needed to clear outgoing checks.
  
> "One of the cheapest ways to meet those reserve requirements, according to Resilience, a program under the Post Carbon Institute, is through “retail deposits” — that’s the money you keep in your savings account. If they can’t attract new customers to borrow cheap retail deposits from, banks have to meet their cash reserve requirements by paying more to borrow wholesale deposits from the Fed. 
  
> In short, banks don’t take the money that you deposit, turn around and loan it at a higher interest rate. But they do use the money you deposit to balance their books and meet the necessary cash reserves that make those loans possible." - Which means the banks use your deposit to make themselves look good to investors.

### **@JeffreyHunn-m1e**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VbowBTJWKBo&lc=UgyQ9x7trngxiYpW46p4AaABAg)

as for "all economic collapses/busts" being engineered, if that is the proposal, then the next issue is how one defines "economic." 

Do we find population collapses in a certain species (within some region) simply because they eat their food source and then starve? What about booms and busts in the gold rush? Once the mine is empty or the entire forest has been cut down, doesn't the boom "naturally" end?

Manic speculation is encouraged by things like systemic incentives (like tax laws) and promotional campaigns. Manias or hysterias of speculation are not designed to be sustainable, but to lead to "buying exhaustion." 

In other words, cycles of economic activity, including as simple as wintertime and summertime cycles of agricultural activity, includes patterns that are intrinsically natural. Further, the engineering of booms and busts by organized small groups... is also "perfectly natural." Why wouldn't farmers or freemasons or pirates form groups to advance their interests relative to competing interests? It is only natural.

### **@viethoango8833** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VbowBTJWKBo&lc=UgxARnudS7vJpPAeJyZ4AaABAg)

1. The Economic Cycle is Not a Mystery
The speaker claims that economics cannot explain why bubbles burst and suggests that an elite group "pulls the strings." This is incorrect.

Economics provides clear explanations: One of the most famous theories is the "Financial Instability Hypothesis" by Hyman Minsky. He pointed out that during periods of stability, people become complacent and borrow excessively for speculation (such as subprime mortgages), leading to high systemic risk. Bubbles burst when cash flow is insufficient to repay debt. It is a consequence of herd mentality and greed, not a secret button being pressed.

The Role of Interest Rates: Interest rates directly impact consumers and businesses. When mortgage rates rise from 3% to 7%, millions of people stop buying homes because monthly costs become too high. This is a basic law of supply and demand, not an "illusion."

2. The 2008 Crash was Systemic Greed, Not a Script
The lecture argues that the 2008 crisis was "designed" so that individuals like John Paulson or firms like JP Morgan could profit.

The Reality: The 2008 crisis resulted from loose regulation (specifically the repeal of the Glass-Steagall Act), the insatiable greed of financial institutions, and the complexity of derivatives (CDOs).

The Overlooked Fact: If this were a perfectly controlled script, why did Lehman Brothers—one of America's oldest and most powerful investment banks—go completely bankrupt? Why did Bear Stearns collapse? The global financial system truly teetered on the brink of total collapse, and governments had to pump in trillions of dollars to save it—a move whose full consequences even the bankers did not entirely foresee.

3. China’s Rise is Due to Internal Factors, Not the BIS
The speaker claims the Bank for International Settlements (BIS) manipulated exchange rates to "pump up" the Chinese economy to replace the U.S. after 2008.

Actual History: The miracle of the Chinese economy began with Deng Xiaoping’s "Reform and Opening-up" in 1978 and gained its strongest momentum when they joined the WTO in 2001 (long before the 2008 crisis). A "golden" demographic dividend, cheap labor costs, and state capitalism were the primary drivers turning China into the "world's factory," not a rate coordination order from Switzerland. China’s central bank (PBOC) strictly controls the Yuan's exchange rate to support exports; the BIS does not do this.

4. The Illogic of "Shifting the Focus to Israel" 
This is the weakest and most speculative point in the lecture.

Economic Scale: Israel’s economy is highly advanced in technology, but its scale (GDP of just over $500 billion) is far too small to become a "center of gravity" replacing the U.S. ($27 trillion) or China.

The Nature of Capital: Global capital is extremely risk-averse. Capital seeks stability, transparent legal environments, and long-term profitability. While war can enrich a small group of defense contractors (the military-industrial complex), it destroys real assets and causes inflation, weakening the macroeconomy. No transnational capital flow would want to concentrate entirely in a region of permanent geopolitical conflict with the hope of getting rich from the "ruins."

5. Distortion of Philosophical History
The speaker alleges that great thinkers like John Locke, David Hume, Karl Marx, and Charles Darwin were funded to create an ideology where "Money is God."

The Truth: Karl Marx was the harshest critic of capitalism in history. His doctrine (Marxism) aimed at overthrowing the very financial bourgeoisie mentioned in the lecture. Lumping Marx together with those "creating illusions to protect bankers" is a complete distortion of fundamental knowledge.

This lecture succeeds in identifying real symptoms: inequality, corporate greed, legislative loopholes, and the fact that certain individuals always profit during crises.

However, its diagnosis is wrong. Instead of viewing the world as a complex, chaotic ecosystem driven by billions of decentralized decisions from humans, politicians, and markets, the speaker simplifies it into a chess game controlled by a few people in the shadows. Believing in a conspiracy theory is often easier than accepting that the world operates chaotically and that no one truly has full control of the wheel.

Cruciaal in 2026: de paus (Francis) heeft recent een "geheime audiëntie" met WEF-leiders in Genève gehad (via BIS), waar symbolen als de dubbele adelaar (33°-teken) opdoken. Het is een brug: Vaticaan als "farao-priesterkaste" die Templar-erfenis beheert. Zwitserland ik het land waar de tempaliers wonen en alles besturen Banken en finance: BIS (Basel), UBS/Credit Suisse (nu UBS), en talloze private banks. Zwitserland is nog steeds de #1 voor "offshore" wealth (hoewel EU/USA druk uitoefenen).NGO's en wereldcomités: Genève: Hoofdkwartier van ICRC (Rode Kruis), WHO, WTO, UNHCR, ILO en bijna alle VN-gerelateerde organisaties buiten New York. WEF (Davos/Cologny): Young Global Leaders, elites die "global governance" pushen. Maar ook CERN (deeltjesfysica, "poort naar nieuwe werelden"), en talloze think tanks. Waarom Zwitserland? Neutraliteit sinds 1815 (Congres van Wenen), Alpen als natuurlijke vesting, en die "verborgen kennis" van de Tempeliers (bankieren, militair, diplomatie). Ook de naam bank komt van de eerste bank die begonnen is door de tempaliers op de rivierbank van londen. Zwitserland is nog steeds de hub: in januari 2026 was er recorddeelname (400 leiders, 65 staatshoofden) onder "A Spirit of Dialogue". SwiSS (ja met SS aan het einde....) politie kreeg speciale grensmacht (entry bans, passenger lists) een fort voor elites. Cruciaal het logo van WEF 2026 heeft een cirkel met driehoek (3+4-piramide) en blauwe accenten (oorlog/verovering). 3+4 is ook 7 en G is de 7e letter... Trump, Macron, von der Leyen en allemaal doen ze de ''pyramid-hands'', "Roman Hands", ''pyramide-voeten stand'', "een oog symbolic of de vleugels van horus" en ''de hand in de jas zoals napoleon'' in foto's. Het is geen toeval Davos is de moderne "tempel" waar farao-rituelen (netwerken, symbolen) doorgaan. Waarom Zwitseland? 1 De timing (Templar-vervolging 1307, opkomst Zwitserse Confederatie rond 1315) 2 De symboliek (omgekeerde kruisen, rood-wit) 3 De functie (bankieren, neutraliteit, geheime netwerken). Zelfs hitlers logo's op oorlog voortuigen was eerst een wit kruis en heeft daar later nog een zwart kruis extra bovenop gemaakt zodat het minder opviel dat zwitseland erachter zat. Tempaliers brachten het geloof naar de rest van de wereld via de kruis tochten en sinds die tijd hebben we koningshuizen. Als je kijkt naar hun familiewapens dan zie je kruisen, slangen en leeuwen. Leeuwen komen nieteens voor in europa en toen die tijd werd er niet veer gereist dus waarom hebben ze dat dan in hun logo's? Waarom al die obelisken? (pharao's natuurlijk). Waarom al die slangen? (Het zijn geen slangen maar het is de hoornadder of Cerastes-adder (Cerastes cerastes) is een giftige slang uit de familie adders Viperidae). De Tempeliers werden in 1307 massaal gearresteerd in Frankrijk (op vrijdag 13 oktober, vandaar de bijgelovigheid). Paus Clemens V ontbond de orde in 1312. Veel ridders vluchtten en naar Schotland (Rosslyn Chapel), Portugal (Orde van Christus), maar ook naar de Zwitserse Alpen. Bestudeer de tekst van het nederlandse volkslied eens goed want daar hoor je van Duitsebloed en koning van spanje altijd geëerd enzo. Velen kennen de lyrics maar begrijpen er niks van..

### **@ApexAca** | 👍 2 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VbowBTJWKBo&lc=UgyWx-VUUraBBXjolnd4AaABAg)

The backlash wasn’t really about the title itself. It was about what it challenged.

Most people move through structured paths to gain recognition. 

So when someone like Professor Jiang gains influence without fully following that structure according to them, it creates a dilemma. For those who have invested heavily in that path, this can feel like a threat. 

Someone who can step outside the system, analyze it, and deconstruct it holds a different kind of authority, one that isn’t dependent on hierarchy or formal validation. And in that sense, the ability to transcend a system carries more weight than simply rising within it, no matter how high that position may be.

People often forget that these fields, systems, and titles are all constructed. They were created by people and then passed down for others to follow. That doesn’t mean they are meaningless, but it does mean they are not absolute.

What really matters is not just the title someone holds, but the substance behind it, their understanding, clarity, depth, and honesty.

Someone can be unconventional, like Professor Jiang, and still be genuine and knowledgeable. Unconventional doesn’t mean untruthful. 

it’s more meaningful to look at the qualities that actually define a person of knowledge.

So when someone takes an unconventional path, the criticism isn’t always about their knowledge, it’s about their failure to fit the accepted image of authority.

Usury is the cancer in economics 
The Ebola virus in capitalism which causes inflation, ends the fair dynamic creation and distribution of wealth in society and is the heart of the ponzy scheme that collapses the economy cyclically. As money concentrates in hard of the few at the top to the point it improvises the population base, it disrupts the balance and the economy collapses under its own weight 


The interest-based economy collapses because it generates structural imbalance. Wealth concentrates upward into fewer hands, debt compounds faster than the real economy grows, and the system becomes too top-heavy, too extractive, and too detached from productive reality to remain stable. It eventually breaks under the weight of its own distortions.

But the deeper mechanism is not just “wealth at the top.” It is this:
	•	money earns without sharing risk,
	•	debt grows regardless of real output,
	•	finance expands faster than production,
	•	the lower and middle layers are drained to sustain the upper layer,
	•	and the whole system requires endless growth to service prior claims.

That creates a fatal pattern:
concentration, fragility, bubbles, crisis, bailout, and deeper concentration again.

So the stronger formulation is:

An interest-based economy contains a built-in law of disequilibrium: it systematically transfers wealth upward, inflates financial claims beyond real production, and makes the system increasingly unstable, until it either crashes openly or survives by crushing the society beneath it.

### **@Corvin2696**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VbowBTJWKBo&lc=UgxHJMBxwb9k_5HMSlJ4AaABAg)

That's not how banks work at all. Let's say I bring $1Mil to the bank for a year. The bank would take that money and bring it to the central bank as collateral to take out a loan. The equity ratio usually has to be around 10% although it is often much less. Equity ratio means how much money a bank can take out for it's collateral. With a ratio of 10%, the bank can take out $10Mil in this case. The bank now lends this money to 10 borrowers for $1 million each, charging interest. At an interest rate of 5% and a term of one year, the bank would receive a total of $10.5 million back from its borrowers at the end of the term. The bank would then have to repay the loan it had from the central bank, including interest at 2%. This would amount to a payment of $10.2 million. The bank receives its initial deposit of $1 million back from the central bank. The saver receives their money back at 1% interest, amounting to $1.01 million. After repaying all debts, the bank is left with $290,000 after one year. This sum is a risk premium that the bank has borne throughout the year and will be used if necessary in the event of borrower defaults (if no default happens, it will become the banks equity). So the margin is very thin, if only one borrower out of the ten defaults on only 30% of his loan (only pays back 700k), the bank is 60k under water. Bust is when too many people defaulted on their loans and the banks equity gets wiped out. It then is no longer able to to give out loans which slows down the economy. Saying it is some sort of conspiracy, is like saying that the four seasons are one too. Mr. Jiang, please start valuing inductive thinking more, because that is the exact problem why you don't get these simple mechanisms. You try to come up with theories how the noumenon might work while completely ignoring that the phenomena speak against your theories and that for understanding the workings of things inductive thinking is much more valuable. Because this is a blind spot for you, this will regularly be a problem for you. Read C.G. Jungs book "Psychological Types" to understand what you overlook.

### **@jacobrodefeld**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VbowBTJWKBo&lc=UgwJDMavLYpCI_c2Pt54AaABAg)

i agree with most of what was said in this video, however I think the idea that the boom bust cycle is inherent to capitalism as is inferred here is incorrect.  It is correct that modern capitalism with central banking and fractional reserve banking DOES produce a boom bust cycle that is manipulated by and for the Elite.  But there is another tradition of economics known as the Austrian School that claims that the cause of the boom bust cycle is the central bank artificially setting interest rates that provoke investment and misallocation of capital goods into unsustainable projects.  Basically, "capital" is disconnected from actual savings and is replaced with artificial credit.  The Austrian economists argue for a non-State commodity money like Gold with a 100% reserve requirement for banks (no fractional reserve banking) and this would produce a clean free market economy without the manipulation of the Old Money Elites that Professor Jiang accurately critiques.
